# hookdoctor contract

This contract is the executable spec for hookdoctor. Every obligation below maps to at least one named test in the test suite. An obligation is met only when its tests pass.

## Ground and probes

Hookdoctor audits the harness's hooks, blindgate audits CI's checks. Hookdoctor diffs a configured set of hooks against execution traces from session files and classifies every configured hook. It reads local files only and never reaches a network in the tool or in its tests.

The configuration probe used a synthetic settings document in the shape the Claude Code harness reads. That document carries a hooks object whose keys are event names and whose values are lists of matcher groups. A matcher group carries an optional matcher string and a hooks list and each entry in that list carries a type and a command. The probe found the events PreToolUse and PostToolUse and Stop in document order. It found a group with the matcher Bash and a group with the matcher Edit|Write and a group with an empty matcher and a Stop group with no matcher field at all. A group with no matcher field and a group with an empty matcher both stand for every subject of their event.

The matcher probe applied the harness rule to synthetic tool names. A matcher with no character outside letters and digits and underscores is a plain name and it matches only the identical tool name and the match is case sensitive, so Bash does not match bash. A matcher carrying any other character is a regular expression and it must match the whole tool name, so Edit|Write matches Edit and matches Write and matches nothing else. The empty matcher and the star matcher match every tool name.

The session probe used synthetic lines in the JSONL shape the harness writes for a session. Each line is one JSON object carrying a type and a timestamp and a session identifier. An assistant line carries message content blocks and a tool use block carries a tool name and a call identifier and an input object. A user line can carry tool result blocks and each one names the call it answers and carries an error flag. An attachment line can carry a hook record whose type is hook_success or hook_blocking_error or hook_non_blocking_error and which names a hook event and the call it ran against and a content string and an exit code. A system line with the subtype stop_hook_summary carries a hook count and hook entries naming commands and a prevented continuation flag and an output flag. The probe read all five line kinds in that shape and no other source was consulted, because no real session file may enter this repo.

The effect ground is fixed by what a trace can show. A trace shows an effect when a hook stopped work or changed work or fed output into the session. A stopped tool call and a prevented continuation are stops. A hook output that carries replacement input for the call it ran against is a change. Output is consumed when a hook record carries context text the session adds, or when a hook output names additional context, or when a stop summary says output stood. A hook that ran and left none of those marks in the trace changed nothing the trace can show, whatever it may have done outside the session.

## Setup obligations

Obligation SETUP-1 covers the seed state. README.md keeps the seed description and licence sections and LICENSE keeps the MIT text and the seed ignore rules stay in place while the status section records later build work. Proven by test_seed_files_present.

Obligation SETUP-2 covers the agent instructions. AGENTS.md begins with the canonical template exactly as supplied for this program and its annex states the boundary sentence and the local file and synthetic fixture and no network rule and the local gate and the rule that the closed round count in README.md moves only at a closure. Proven by test_agents_template_and_annex.

## Slice 2 obligations

Obligation HD2-1 covers configuration ingestion. The ingester reads a settings document and walks its hooks object in document order and flattens every matcher group into one record per hook entry. A record carries the event and the matcher text or none when the group names none and the hook type and the command and a stable identifier built from the event and the group position and the entry position. An entry carrying no type field reads as a command entry. A matcher that is not a plain name is compiled while the configuration is ingested and a matcher that is not a valid pattern is refused there, whatever event it stands under and whatever any trace holds. A document with no hooks object yields no hooks and that is not an error. A malformed document and a group whose hooks list is missing and an entry with no command are refused with a hookdoctor error rather than guessed. Proven by test_config_flattens_groups_in_document_order and test_config_without_hooks_yields_no_hooks and test_malformed_config_is_refused.

Obligation HD2-2 covers trace ingestion. The ingester reads one or more session files and parses each nonempty line as one JSON object and refuses a line that is not valid JSON and a line whose object carries no type. From those lines it builds dated records in file order. A tool call record comes from a tool use block and carries the tool name and the call identifier and the timestamp and the session and the line number. A tool outcome record comes from a tool result block and carries the answered call identifier and the error flag and the result text. A prompt record comes from a user line whose content is text and not a tool result. A hook run record comes from a hook attachment whose fields name the event and the call and the hook and its content and its exit code. A stop record comes from a stop hook summary line and carries its commands and its continuation and output flags. The event of a hook run record is its event field when one stands and otherwise the part of its hook name before the first colon. A hook attachment whose fields name no event at all names no event, and the examination builds no subject from such a record, so the record stands outside the examination. Lines of any other type are counted and carried as other records and never guessed into evidence. Proven by test_trace_builds_dated_records_from_each_line_kind and test_trace_refuses_a_line_that_is_not_json and test_trace_refuses_a_line_with_no_type.

Beside text note of slice 12. The sentence standing beside the derivation sentence above, that a hook attachment whose fields name no event at all names no event and that the examination builds no subject from such a record so the record stands outside the examination, stands superseded in two standings and stands word for word by design. Slice 3 places an eventless record as a subject where the hooks it identifies all subscribe to one event, under EV-2, citing this sentence only for the residual case where placement is impossible, and slice 9 places the eventless record that identifies nothing inside the unconfigured runs list, under EU-2, which is inside the examination by any reading, and slice 11 left the sentence untouched word for word and said so in its preamble, and this note names the supersession where the sentence stands and repairs no word of it.

Obligation HD2-3 covers matching. A configured hook subscribes to one event. A tool call is the subject of PreToolUse and its outcome is the subject of PostToolUse and a prompt is the subject of UserPromptSubmit and a stop record is the subject of Stop and a hook run record is the subject of the event it names. A hook matches a subject when the event agrees and the matcher agrees with the subject tool name under the probed rule, where an event with no tool subject agrees with every matcher. A hook run record agrees with a hook when the event agrees and the record identifies that hook by its command or by its own name form, where the name form is the event alone or the event joined to the hook matcher or the command standing alone as the name. The event alone is the name form of a hook whose matcher is empty or missing and of no other hook, and a name form carrying one hook matcher never identifies a sibling hook on the same event. A record carrying both a command and a name form agrees only when both identify the same hook, and a record whose two identifiers point at different hooks agrees with no hook, and a record carrying one identifier is decided by that one. Proven by test_plain_matcher_is_exact_and_case_sensitive and test_regex_matcher_must_match_the_whole_name and test_empty_and_missing_matchers_match_every_tool and test_non_tool_events_agree_with_every_matcher.

Obligation HD2-4 covers a hook that ran. A hook ran on a subject when it matches that subject and the trace shows the harness reached it. A PreToolUse hook reaches every tool call it matches. A PostToolUse hook reaches a matched call only when that call has an outcome record. A UserPromptSubmit hook reaches every prompt it matches. A Stop hook reaches a stop record that names its command among its hook entries. Any hook also ran on a hook run record that agrees with it. A hook that never ran on any subject in the window did not fire in the window. Proven by test_pretool_hook_runs_on_a_matched_call and test_posttool_hook_needs_an_outcome_to_have_run and test_stop_hook_runs_when_the_summary_names_its_command and test_hook_run_record_counts_as_a_run.

Obligation HD2-5 covers the effect definition. An effect is a denial or a modification or consumed output and nothing else counts. A denial stands when a matched call carries an outcome whose error flag is set and whose text names a denial under the denial reading or names the hook command, or when a hook run record agreeing with the hook is a blocking error, or when a hook run record carries output whose decision field is block or whose permission decision field is deny, or when a stop record that names the hook has its continuation prevented. A modification stands when a hook run record agreeing with the hook carries output with replacement input for its call. Replacement input is the change by definition, because the trace carries no original input for a call, so a replacement is never compared against an original the trace does not hold and a replacement input identical to what the call would have carried still counts as a modification. Consumed output stands when a hook run record agreeing with the hook carries nonempty context content, or carries output naming additional context text, or when a stop record that names the hook says output stood. Content carried by an error record, whether a blocking error or a non blocking error, is never consumed output, because an error record reports the run itself and adds no context to the session. The denial reading of an outcome text is fixed and narrow. The text names a denial when it carries the denial word, and a text that negates its denial in the plain forms, with the word not standing before the denial word or a contracted not standing before it or the word never standing before it, names no denial at all. A text that attributes its denial to a named party, in the shape of the denial word followed by the word by and a name shaped token carrying a hyphen or a dot or an underscore or a slash, credits the hook whose command that party is when the party names a configured hook and credits no hook when the party names none, and while an attribution stands the unattributed reading does not apply. A denial text naming no party credits every hook matching the call, and that crediting is a stated limit of outcome text evidence, because the text cannot say which hook spoke and an operating system permission error standing in an outcome text reads exactly the same way. Output that is never carried by a record agreeing with the hook is not an effect of that hook, and an ordinary successful outcome is not an effect. Proven by test_denial_by_outcome_text_and_by_blocking_record and test_denial_by_output_decision_and_by_prevented_continuation and test_modification_by_replacement_input and test_consumed_output_by_context_and_additional_context and test_plain_success_is_no_effect.

Obligation HD2-6 covers the three classes. Every configured hook receives exactly one class. A hook that ran and shows at least one effect is firing. A hook that ran and shows no effect is firing without effect. A hook that never ran is silent. The class never depends on another hook and never on the order hooks stand in the configuration. Proven by test_firing_hook_with_a_denial and test_hook_that_ran_on_every_event_and_changed_nothing_is_without_effect and test_silent_hook_matched_nothing and test_every_hook_gets_exactly_one_class.

Obligation HD2-7 covers evidence. Each hook entry in the report carries the count of subjects it matched and the count of subjects it ran on and the count of effects by kind and the earliest and latest timestamp among the subjects it ran on, read as instants so two spellings of one instant are one date and the date stands spelled as the trace spelled it, or no dates when it never ran. A timestamp that cannot be read as an instant takes no part in the dates. The evidence is counted from the trace records alone and two runs over the same inputs carry the same evidence. Proven by test_evidence_counts_and_dates_come_from_the_trace and test_hook_that_never_ran_has_no_dates.

Amendment of slice 22, standing beside the obligation it amends. The evidence obligation above states the earliest and latest timestamp among the subjects a hook ran on, read as instants so two spellings of one instant are one date and the date stands spelled as the trace spelled it, and those words do not name which spelling stands where two spellings of one instant stand at an extreme among the ran subjects. Slice 22 reads the choice whole, the first date standing as the smallest spelling of the earliest instant in the order of the text and the last date as the largest spelling of the latest instant, a spelling standing between them standing as neither date, and the choice standing unmoved by the order the subjects stand in the trace, and the obligation stands otherwise exactly as written, this paragraph amending it in that one reading and leaving its words standing.

Obligation HD2-8 covers near misses. Near misses are computed only for silent hooks and are a separate list and never change a class. A traced subject in the window is a near miss for a silent hook under one of three stated edits. The name form edit stands when the matcher is a plain name and a tool name in the same event differs from it only by letter case or only by the choice between a hyphen and an underscore and a joined form. The one letter edit stands when the matcher is a plain name and a tool name in the same event is one insertion or one deletion or one substitution away from it. The event edit stands when the subject carries the paired event of the hook event, meaning PreToolUse against PostToolUse in either direction for a tool the matcher matches, or when the subject event name is the hook event name with the prefix Subagent added or removed. Each near miss names the hook and the subject and the edit that applies and the timestamp and the line and the session source the subject came from. The same subject standing on the same line of two session files yields two near misses, one for each source. A subject that matches the hook outright is never a near miss. Proven by test_near_miss_by_name_form and test_near_miss_by_one_letter and test_near_miss_by_paired_event and test_near_miss_by_subagent_prefix and test_a_match_is_never_a_near_miss_and_near_misses_change_no_class.

Obligation HD2-9 covers the window. The examination may carry a window start and a window end as timestamps. A subject counts only when its timestamp stands at or after the start and before the end, and a bound that is not given excludes nothing on its side. Timestamps and bounds are read as instants, so a record spelled with an offset stands inside a bound spelled in universal time when the instants agree, and a timestamp that cannot be read as an instant stands outside every window that carries a bound, and a bound that cannot be read as an instant is refused with a hookdoctor error. Matching and running and effects and near misses are all judged inside the window alone, so a hook whose only matches stand outside the window is silent in the window and its near misses are the subjects inside the window that nearly matched. A record with no timestamp stands outside every window that carries a bound. Proven by test_match_outside_the_window_leaves_the_hook_silent and test_near_miss_inside_the_window_is_shown_for_an_outside_match and test_unbounded_window_counts_everything.

Obligation HD2-10 covers the report format. The format name is hookdoctor-report/1. The report carries the format name and the configuration source label and the session source labels and the window and one entry per configured hook in configuration order and the near miss list. Each entry carries the stable hook identifier and the event and the matcher and the command and the hook type and the class and the evidence of HD2-7. Each near miss carries the hook identifier and the reason and the event and the tool and the timestamp and the session source and the line and the detail. The class words are firing and silent and firing-without-effect and those words are the join keys a later slice hands to the ledger as evidence. A later slice joining verdicts to the ledger keys on the event and the matcher and the command and the class word, because the stable hook identifier is positional inside one configuration document and moves when the document moves. Validation rebuilds the expected report from the same inputs and refuses a supplied report when any field differs or when a field is missing or when an unexpected field stands in the report. Proven by test_report_carries_the_format_and_one_entry_per_hook and test_report_validation_refuses_a_tampered_class and test_report_validation_refuses_tampered_evidence_and_metadata.

Obligation HD2-11 covers deterministic output and the offline boundary. The command line requires one local configuration file and at least one local session file and it writes one JSON report and one markdown report. JSON output uses sorted keys and two space indentation and one trailing newline. Entries stand in configuration order and near misses stand in a stable order by hook and timestamp and line. No output carries a wall clock time or a local filesystem path, because source labels are the labels the caller gives or the file names alone. Running the command twice on the same inputs produces byte identical report bytes. The tool reads only the local files named on its command line and a missing local input is refused with a hookdoctor error. The test suite builds every fixture synthetically and uses no network. Proven by test_cli_writes_deterministic_outputs and test_missing_inputs_are_refused and test_outputs_carry_no_local_paths.

Amendment of slice 21, standing beside the obligation it amends. The deterministic output obligation above states the near miss order as an order by hook and timestamp and line, and those words do not name the whole key the sort runs on, the source standing between the instant and the line in it and a timestamp that does not read standing at the earliest instant. Slice 21 reads the order whole, the near misses standing in a stable order by hook and by instant and by source and by line, a miss whose timestamp does not read as an instant standing at the earliest instant of the grammar, and a miss carrying no source standing in that order at the empty text, and the obligation stands otherwise exactly as written, this paragraph amending it in that one reading and leaving its words standing.

## Round 1 review fixes

The Round 1 red team returned a conditional verdict on slice 2 with two major findings and four minor findings and three notes, and the Round 1 two axis review returned a conditional verdict on each axis with one major finding on the spec axis and one minor finding on the standards axis, and the obligations below record the repairs. They amend HD2-1 and HD2-2 and HD2-3 and HD2-5 and HD2-7 and HD2-8 and HD2-9 and HD2-10 where they conflict, and the amended sentences stand in those obligations themselves. The report format name stays hookdoctor-report/1 and the one format change is the session source field the near miss entries gain under FIX-4.

Obligation FIX-1 covers the denial reading of outcome text and amends HD2-5. The reading is the one HD2-5 now states. A negated denial credits no hook. A denial attributed to a name shaped party credits the hook whose command that party is and credits no hook when the party names no configured hook, and the unattributed crediting of every matching hook stands as a named limit in HD2-5 and in the README. Proven by test_negated_denial_text_credits_no_hook and test_denial_attributed_to_an_unconfigured_party_credits_no_hook and test_ambient_denial_text_credits_every_matching_hook and test_denial_attributed_to_a_configured_hook_credits_only_that_hook.

Obligation FIX-2 covers the content of error records and amends HD2-5. Content carried by a blocking error record or by a non blocking error record never counts as consumed output, while the denial a blocking error record carries by its kind stands. Proven by test_non_blocking_error_content_is_not_consumed_output and test_blocking_error_content_is_not_consumed_output.

Obligation FIX-3 covers run record agreement and amends HD2-3. A run record identifies a hook by the hook command or by the hook own name form alone. The event alone identifies only a hook with an empty or missing matcher. A name form carrying one hook matcher identifies no sibling on the same event. A record whose command and name form point at different hooks identifies no hook at all, and a record carrying one identifier is decided by that one. Proven by test_sibling_hook_is_not_credited_by_another_hooks_run_record and test_bare_event_run_record_credits_only_matcherless_hooks and test_run_record_with_disagreeing_identifiers_credits_no_hook and test_run_record_command_alone_identifies_its_hook.

Obligation FIX-4 covers the PostToolUse subject and the near miss sources and amends HD2-7 and HD2-8 and HD2-10. The PostToolUse subject is the outcome record of its call, so the evidence dates of a PostToolUse hook are the dates of the outcomes it ran on. Each near miss carries the session source its subject came from and near misses deduplicate with the source in the key, so the same line in two session files yields two near misses. Proven by test_posttool_evidence_dates_come_from_the_outcome and test_near_misses_name_their_session_source.

Obligation FIX-5 covers matcher validation and the instant reading and amends HD2-1 and HD2-7 and HD2-9. A matcher that is not a plain name is compiled when the configuration is ingested and an invalid pattern is refused there, whatever the trace holds and whatever event the matcher stands under. Window membership and evidence dates read timestamps as instants, and a bound that cannot be read as an instant is refused. Proven by test_invalid_matcher_pattern_is_refused_at_ingestion and test_window_bounds_compare_as_instants and test_evidence_dates_are_ordered_as_instants.

Obligation FIX-6 covers the pairing of outcomes to calls and states the pairing the subjects of HD2-3 rest on. Where one call identifier stands on several calls, the outcomes answering that identifier pair to the calls in file order, so each call is judged against its own outcome and one denial is counted once for its own call and a denial standing behind an earlier success is not hidden. Proven by test_outcomes_pair_to_calls_in_order.

Obligation FIX-7 covers the small standards repairs that carry no new examination behaviour beyond one matcher reading. The unused hash helper in the ingester is gone and the unread subject field and the unused binding in the examination are gone. A matcher never matches a missing tool name, and the agreement of a tool less subject with every matcher is decided where the subject is matched, in the open, and not inside the matcher. The standing corners are stated where they belong, the replacement input corner in HD2-5 and the event less run record and the event field derivation in HD2-2 and the missing entry type reading in HD2-1. Proven by test_matcher_never_matches_a_missing_tool_name and test_non_tool_events_agree_with_every_matcher.

## Round 2 amendment

The duplicate key probe read a configuration document whose hooks object carried one event key twice, once over a list holding hooks and once over an empty list. The reader decoded the document with the plain JSON decoder and raised nothing, and the ingester flattened the surviving empty list, so every hook standing under the discarded value vanished from the examination while the report read as if those hooks had never been configured. A second probe put a repeated command key inside one hook entry and the second command survived in the same silent way. A third probe put a repeated type key on a session line and the record kind followed the surviving value there too. A fourth probe drove the pair preserving decoder hook the repair uses and found that the hook fires for an inner object before the object that carries it, and that scanning the pairs of one object in written order names the first key that stands twice in that object.

Obligation R2-1 covers duplicate keys in the JSON documents the tool accepts and amends HD2-1 and HD2-2. Every JSON document the tool accepts is decoded with a check that refuses the document when one object in it carries the same key twice. The documents the tool accepts are the configuration document and each session line, and each session line is a document of its own for this rule. The refusal is a hookdoctor error and it names the document and the repeated key, so the probe configuration refuses with the words the configuration carries the key PreToolUse more than once, and the probe session line refuses with the words a session line carries the key type more than once. The check fires for the innermost object first and names the first key that stands twice in written order inside one object, and it covers objects at any depth including objects inside lists. The same key standing in two different objects is not a repeat and reads normally. The output text a hook run record carries is not a document the tool accepts, and the sampling reading of HD2-5 that reads effect fields out of that text keeps its standing reading, where a text that does not read as an object is no output and a repeated key inside a text that does read leaves the last value standing. No other reading behaviour changes. Proven by test_duplicate_keys_in_the_configuration_are_refused and test_duplicate_keys_refuse_at_any_depth_and_name_the_first_repeat and test_duplicate_keys_in_a_session_line_are_refused and test_the_same_key_in_different_objects_is_accepted and test_output_text_keeps_its_standing_sampling_reading.

## Rust port slice 1

The port ground is the landed Python at the Round 2 amendment. The Python package is the spec and the port reproduces its readings exactly on the surface this slice ports. The probes below were run against the Python runtime on the building machine before the port was written.

The JSON probe found that the Python decoder accepts the NaN and Infinity and negative Infinity spellings as numbers and keeps integers exact at any size the runtime conversion limit allows and refuses a raw control character inside a string and accepts a lone surrogate escape as a string holding that surrogate. The same probe found that an integer token beyond the runtime limit of 4300 digits raises a plain value error the command line does not catch, so such a document ends the Python command in a crash rather than a reading. The timestamp probe drove the instant reading of the examination module across the ISO grammar and found the extended and basic date and time forms and the week date form with or without a weekday accepted and the ordinal date form refused and any single character accepted as the separator between the date and the time and an uppercase or lowercase z accepted standing at the end of the text and fractional hours and minutes and seconds accepted with digits beyond six truncated and offsets accepted in the hour only and hour and minute and hour and minute and second forms with or without colons and with a dotted fourth component read as the offset fraction and offset components summed rather than range checked with only the whole size held under a full day and the fraction of an offset whose whole part stands at zero dropped and an empty fraction accepted where a zone stands after it and a reading carrying no offset read as universal time by the examination itself. The rendering probe found that the Python JSON rendering escapes every character outside the printable ASCII range and sorts object keys by code point. The matcher probe found no pattern in the suite or the examples beyond plain names and one alternation of two plain names, and the port grammar below is stated against the wider pattern grammar the harness accepts in principle.

Limits of the port stand stated rather than hidden. A document carrying a lone surrogate escape is refused by the port as not valid JSON, because the Rust text type holds no such string, where the Python reader accepts it. A document nested deeper than 4000 levels is refused by the port with the same not valid JSON refusal, where the Python decoder sets no bound of its own and reads on until the runtime stops it. Integers beyond the 4300 digit runtime limit keep their exact digits in the port where the Python command crashes on them. A hook output text nested deeper than the bound reads as no output in the port, where the Python sampling reading meets the same depth as a crash the tool does not catch. A matcher pattern standing outside the grammar PORT-4 names is refused by the port as not a valid pattern where the Python reading compiles it. A matcher pattern whose counted repetitions break the guard RFIX-3 states is refused by the port on the same side, where the Python reading compiles it and matches with it. In each case the difference stands at a boundary the Python side meets as a crash or as a value no consumer can use or as a pattern no probed configuration carries, and the port takes the conservative side.

Obligation PORT-1 covers the crate shape. The port is a library crate named hookdoctor with a thin binary of the same name, on edition 2021, using only the Rust standard library. The repo gains a continuous integration workflow carrying a cargo job beside a Python job, and the Python job keeps its standing gate command, because no workflow stands in this repo today. The binary carries the examination end to end over the ported modules, reading a configuration file and one or more session files with optional source labels and an optional window and writing the JSON report and the markdown report to the named output paths, and a refusal on that path prints the hookdoctor error line to standard error and exits with the code the Python command uses for a refusal. The argument surface of the binary is the shell's own and is not a port of the Python argument parser and its usage messages.

Obligation PORT-2 covers the JSON reading. The reader reads the same grammar the Python decoder reads, with whitespace and number and string and literal rules matching the probe findings, and it refuses a document carrying the same key twice in one object under obligation R2-1 with the same message naming the document and the repeated key. Integer tokens that fit in a signed 64 bit integer are stored as that integer and every other integer token is stored as its exact digit text and renders back digit for digit, never as a float. The reader bounds nesting at 4000 levels. The sampling reading that reads effect fields out of hook output text uses the same decoder without the duplicate refusal, keeping the standing last value for a repeated key, and a text that does not read as an object samples as no output.

Obligation PORT-3 covers the ingestion readings. The examination consumes no input except through the configuration reading and the session reading of the ingestion module, so those two readings are ported as the substrate this slice stands on, with every refusal in the same words and every record field read the same way, including the hook event a run record derives from its hook name and the truthiness reading that turns a carried value into a flag. The matcher validity check of HD2-1 stands at ingestion in the port as it does in the Python.

Obligation PORT-4 covers the matcher reading. A plain matcher under the probed rule matches only the identical tool name and a matcher that is no plain name is read as a pattern that must match the whole tool name. The pattern grammar the port reads covers literal characters and alternation and grouping with capturing and noncapturing groups and character classes with ranges and negation and the dot and the repetition forms star and plus and question and counted repetition with lazy forms read as their plain forms and the anchors and the character escapes for digits and word characters and whitespace and their negations. Patterns standing outside that grammar, among them backreferences and lookahead and lookbehind and named groups and inline flags and conditionals, are refused at ingestion as not valid patterns. The three denial readings of HD2-5 are fixed patterns and the port builds them directly to the same words, with the same word boundary and negation and attribution readings.

Obligation PORT-5 covers instants. The instant reading first rewrites a trailing uppercase or lowercase z as the zero offset, as the Python examination does before its own reading, and then accepts the grammar the timestamp probe recorded and normalizes every accepted reading to universal time, and a reading carrying no offset stands at universal time and a reading the grammar refuses takes no part in any window or date. Window bounds that cannot be read are refused in the same words as the Python refuses them. Evidence dates compare as instants and stand spelled as the trace spelled them, with two spellings of one instant ordered by their spelling where their instants agree, as the Python tuple ordering orders them.

Obligation PORT-6 covers the examination. The port builds the same subjects with the same outcome pairing in file order and the same matching and running and effect readings and the same three classes and the same evidence and the same near misses under the same three edits, with the run record agreement reading of FIX-3 standing exactly as the owner ruled it, so a record whose two identifiers point at different hooks agrees with no hook. Near misses stand in the same order under the same sort keys and the same stability. Validation rebuilds the expected report from the same inputs and refuses a supplied report that differs in any field, with number equality read the way the Python reads it across the integer and float and truth spellings.

Obligation PORT-7 covers rendering. The JSON rendering is byte for byte the Python rendering with sorted keys and two space indentation and the ASCII escaping the rendering probe recorded and one trailing newline. The markdown rendering is byte for byte the Python rendering, line for line, including the words it prints for a missing matcher and a missing date and a missing source.

Obligation PORT-8 covers the equivalence method. Equivalence is proven by recorded conformance. A probe generator runs the amended landed Python over the inputs its own test suite uses and records the answers, and the committed Rust conformance suite runs those recordings offline and compares the reader answers and the configuration answers and the session answers and the instant answers and the sampled output answers and the examination reports byte for byte or message for message. The recordings are never edited by hand. A change to a recorded answer without a matching change in the Python spec fails the suite.

The unported inventory stands as follows. The command line module whole stays Python only in this slice, and with it the argument parsing and its usage messages and the writing of outputs at the Python command. The Python suite stands as the spec and stays in place.

The bindings judgment is that this slice carries no Python bindings. Every consumer of the examination meets its readings across a process boundary as files, the configuration and session files in and the two reports out, through the Python command line today and the binary door in the port, and the verdicts that join the ledger in a later slice travel as report documents, so no consumer shape justifies a binding. The non Rust remainder is the Python package and its suite, which stand as the spec this slice is proven against, and each later slice retires the Python module it ports.

The end of slice line share stands counted at the final head of the slice, with blank lines and comment only lines excluded and the recordings counted on neither side as data. The crate carries 3227 lines of Rust in its modules and 467 lines in the recorded conformance suite, and the Python package carries 885 lines, so the ported surface stands at 81 percent Rust with the conformance suite counted as testing and at 78 percent Rust on production lines alone. What remains Python only is the command line module named in the unported inventory and the Python suite of 1357 lines standing as the spec.

## Round 2 review fixes

The Round 2 red team returned a conditional verdict on the port slice with three major findings and four minor findings, and the Round 2 two axis review returned a conditional verdict on the spec axis with one minor finding and an approve verdict on the standards axis, and the obligations below record the repairs. They amend HD2-5 and PORT-2 and PORT-4 where they conflict, and the limits paragraph of the port section gains the repetition guard sentence. The report format name stays hookdoctor-report/1 and no Python reading changes in this pass.

Obligation RFIX-1 covers the denial attribution party shape and amends HD2-5 and FIX-1. The party a denial is attributed to is a name shaped token carrying a hyphen or a dot or an underscore or a slash, and the underscore stands in that set in the port as it stands in the Python, so a denial attributed to a party whose only shape character is an underscore stands as an attribution in the port and the unattributed crediting of every hook matching the call no longer fires for such a text, where the port before this repair read such a party as no attribution at all and credited the denial to hooks the text never named. The recorded examination cases named for the underscore parties carry the reading.

Obligation RFIX-2 covers possessive quantifiers and amends PORT-4. A quantifier followed by a plus sign is a possessive form, and a pattern carrying a possessive form is refused at ingestion as not a valid pattern, in the family of refusals PORT-4 names, where the Python reading compiles the pattern and reads it with possessive semantics and so matches a different set of names than the plain form would. The lazy forms keep their standing reading as the plain forms, and no other quantifier reading changes. The recorded boundary cases named for the possessive forms carry the refusal.

Obligation RFIX-3 covers the repetition guard and amends PORT-4. A counted repetition stands refused where its count is written with more than six digits or where its count stands past 10000. A pattern stands refused where its expansion passes 1000000 instructions. The expansion counts the instructions a compilation emits, with a counted repetition emitting its body once for each count it carries and one split more for each copy past its lowest count, and an unbounded repetition emitting its body once more with a split and a jump, and each alternation branch but the last emitting a split and a jump. The guard stands because nested counted repetitions multiply as they expand, and the pattern (a{10000}){10000} expands to one hundred million instructions and ends the expanding process in a failed allocation where the Python reading compiles it and answers at once, while the largest expansion the recorded surface carries stands at ninety thousand instructions and reads as it always did. The refusal is the matcher refusal of HD2-1 and it stands at ingestion. The recorded boundary cases named for the per count cap and the expansion ceiling and the recorded matcher cases for the nested forms inside the guard carry it.

Obligation RFIX-4 covers a lone surrogate unit in hook output text and amends PORT-2. The sampling reading tolerates a lone surrogate unit in the text it samples, as the Python decoder tolerates it, and the port reads such a unit as the Unicode replacement character. The fields the sampling reading reads out of an output object are the decision word and the permission decision word and the standing of replacement input and the standing and emptiness of additional context, and a replacement character spells neither decision word and empties no text and fills no empty text, so no fact the sampling reading takes from a text changes under the substitution. The refusal of a lone surrogate escape in the documents the tool accepts stands unchanged, and a text nested deeper than the standing bound still reads as no output. The recorded examination cases named for the surrogate output texts carry the reading.

Obligation RFIX-5 covers the digit character escape and amends PORT-4. The digit escape and its negation match exactly the characters standing in the Unicode decimal number category, inside a character class as outside one, and the category stands in the crate as a table of code point ranges generated from the unicodedata of the Python runtime the spec runs on, which is CPython 3.12 over Unicode 15.0.0. A superscript two and a circled digit one match the negated digit escape and not the digit escape, and an Arabic Indic digit matches the digit escape. The recorded matcher cases for the digit escape against those characters carry the reading.

Obligation RFIX-6 covers the letter z escape and amends PORT-4. A backslash followed by the letter z stands outside the pattern grammar, and a pattern carrying it is refused as not a valid pattern, as the Python reading refuses it as a bad escape, and the capital letter Z escape keeps its standing reading as the end anchor. The recorded matcher case for the refused escape carries it.

Obligation RFIX-7 covers the octal escapes and amends PORT-4. A backslash followed by digits reads as the Python reads it. A first digit of zero opens an octal escape that reads up to three digits in all. A nonzero first digit followed by two octal digits reads as an octal escape of three digits, and it stands refused where its value passes 255, as the Python reading refuses it there. Any other run of digits after a backslash is a group reference and stands refused in the family of refusals PORT-4 names, whether or not a group of that number stands in the pattern. Inside a character class a backslash followed by an octal digit reads as an octal escape of up to three digits under the same value bound, and a backslash followed by the digit eight or the digit nine inside a class stands refused as not a valid pattern, as the Python reading refuses it as a bad escape. The recorded matcher cases for the octal forms carry the readings.

## Rust port slice 2, the closing slice

The obligations below are the closing slice of the port, where the command line is rebuilt as the Python's own and the Python retires. They amend two standing texts and leave each standing so the record shows both. The argument surface sentence of PORT-1, under which the binary's argument surface is the shell's own and not a port of the Python argument parser, is amended here, because that standing was a slice convenience and the closing slice carries the parser's model whole. The inventory sentence of slice 1, under which the command line module whole stays Python only, is amended as the slice it waited for. The examination the binary carries end to end under PORT-1 stands unchanged in its readings; what changes is the shell around it. The landed Python stands as the behavioural specification, and the probes recorded below were run against it before these obligations were written. At the end of this slice the Python is gone from the tree, and the recordings stand as the specification from then on.

A probe of the command line surface ran the landed Python over its own edges and recorded the following. The command line is one flat parser with no subcommands. The configuration flag and the output and report flags are required and stand once, and the session flag is required and repeats, each standing adding one session file in the order given, and the session label flag repeats in the same way. A refusal of the argument reading exits with code 2, and a refusal of the tool also exits with code 2 under the one line carrying the hookdoctor error prefix, the two classes told apart by the standard error text alone. A long flag may stand shortened to any prefix that names one flag alone, and the probe records that the natural shortenings of this parser collide, the configuration prefix and the session prefix each naming two flags and refusing as ambiguous, while the output and report prefixes and the label suffixes read. The committed example outputs regenerate through the Python command line byte for byte. A label that does not stand reads as the file name of its path as the platform path reading takes it, the final component after the last separator of this platform. A probe of the nesting band ran the command line over a configuration carrying a value nested 4500 deep in a field the reading does not read, and the Python completed, because its decoder reads the whole document before the reading chooses its fields. The crate reader refuses such a document at its bound, and that divergence is stated as a limit under PORT2-3 below rather than discovered in review. A probe of the label words ran the command line with a configuration label and with a session label carrying a raw byte that is not UTF-8 text, and the Python died with an uncaught encoding error inside the report rendering, exit code 1, in both cases. A window word carrying such a byte refused cleanly at exit code 2 under the window reading's own wording, because no such word can read as a timestamp.

Obligation PORT2-1 covers the scope and shape of this slice. The binary's shell is rebuilt as the command line of the Python module whole, the one flat door with the flags of PORT2-2 over the library readings slice 1 ported, and the slice 1 shell and its own wordings retire with it. The Python package and its module entry point and the project file are removed from the tree at the end of this slice. No new dependency stands in the crate.

Obligation PORT2-2 covers the argument reading. The flags are the configuration flag, the session flag, the output flag, the report flag, the configuration label flag, the session label flag, and the window start and window end flags, with the required and repeatable standing the probe recorded, the session flag carrying at least one standing or the call refuses as a usage refusal. Every long flag reads in its full spelling or shortened to a prefix that names one flag alone, and reads with its value after an equals sign, and a flag that stands once keeps its last value where it stands more than once, and a repeating flag keeps every standing in the order given, and the cluster and help readings of the parser stand as the earlier slices of this program recorded them, a single dash cluster whose first letter is h firing the help at exit code 0. A missing required flag, a session flag that never stands, a flag with no value, a flag the parser does not carry, an ambiguous shortened flag, and a positional word are each a usage refusal, exiting with code 2 with nothing read and nothing written and nothing on standard output. The texts the Python argument reader writes stand outside parity as the standing divergence of this slice, the binary writing its own short usage line, and what is carried across exactly is the exit code discipline and the refusal line of the tool itself.

Obligation PORT2-3 covers the door. The door reads the configuration under its label through the crate reader and reads its hooks, and then reads each session file in the order given as bytes decoded as UTF-8 text under the session file label, a file whose bytes are not UTF-8 text refusing in those words, and reads its records with the file's own index, and then resolves the source labels, a session label count that does not match the session file count refusing with the wording that session labels do not match the session files, the configuration source reading as the configuration label where it stands and failing that as the file name of the configuration path, and each session source reading the same way from its own label or its own path, and the door builds the report and validates it against its inputs as the Python builds and validates it, and writes the JSON report to the output path and then the markdown report to the report path, a write that fails refusing with the wording that an output could not be written, and a refusal at any point exits with code 2 under the refusal line with the outputs already written standing as the Python leaves them. The window words read through the timestamp reading under their own field wordings, the window start and the window end named in their refusals as the Python names them. A configuration label word or a session label word that is not UTF-8 text is refused at the point the labels are resolved and ahead of every write, under the wordings that the configuration label is not UTF-8 text and that a session label is not UTF-8 text, the Python dying uncaught on such a word, which the refusal translates. Proven by the named Rust pins test_a_configuration_label_carrying_a_non_utf8_byte_is_refused and test_a_session_label_carrying_a_non_utf8_byte_is_refused. A document nested past the reader bound of PORT-2 is refused in the reader wording as not valid JSON under its label, and this stands as a stated port limit in the band the probe recorded. The shell takes its arguments as OS strings end to end and never lossy converts a value it hands to the filesystem, proven by the named Rust pin test_a_file_name_carrying_a_non_utf8_byte_is_read.

Obligation PORT2-4 covers the equivalence method for the shell. Equivalence is proven against recorded Python answers and never against a restatement, under the recorded conformance method slice 1 states. A probe generator kept in the round scratch runs the landed Python command line as a black box over invocations drawn from its own suite and from the argument edges the probe recorded, in scratch directories it makes for the purpose, and records each invocation with its argument list and its input files and its exit code and its standard error text and the files it wrote with their bytes, and the committed Rust test under the name test_cli_battery_matches_the_python_record carries those recordings and runs the binary over the same invocations in its own scratch directories and compares exit codes and standard error texts and written files at content level, and asserts the count of its own sections so a case lost in silence fails the suite. The battery carries the behaviours the Python command line suite pins, the deterministic outputs over the example inputs as paired runs and the committed example outputs standing among them, sessions standing in order under repeated flags with their labels in order, the label defaults taken from the paths including a path whose name carries a backslash as one component on this platform, the window standing open and closed and refused, and the session label count mismatch, beside the argument cases of PORT2-2 and the refusal wordings of the reader under their labels and a write failure under the output wording. ## Slice 3, the eventless run record

The obligations below are the third slice, the first standing on the Rust alone with no Python behind it, the recordings of the earlier slices standing as the specification for everything they cover. They amend one sentence of HD2-2 and leave it standing so the record shows both. HD2-2 derives the event of a hook run record from its event field or from the part of its hook name before the first colon, and states that a hook attachment whose fields name no event at all names no event and that the examination builds no subject from such a record, so the record stands outside the examination. The probes below were run against the built binary before these obligations were written. A hook run record carrying replacement input and naming its hook by command, with no event field and a hook name carrying no colon, leaves its hook standing as firing without effect, because the modification only that record can show is never counted, while the identical record with an event field standing reads as firing with the modification counted. A blocking record in the same standing loses its own denial the same way, the hook credited only with whatever the call and its outcome show without it. The record identifies its hook beyond doubt under the agreement rules of HD2-3 and names no event only because the trace spelled none, and the configuration the record is read against holds exactly the events its hooks subscribe to, so the event the record cannot name is recoverable from the hooks it identifies wherever that recovery is unambiguous.

- EV-1 The scope and shape of this slice. The examination gains the resolution of this section and nothing else moves. The report format hookdoctor-report/1 stands unchanged in shape, every class word and evidence field standing as HD2-10 states, and no new dependency stands in the crate.
- EV-2 The resolution. A hook run record whose event stands derived by neither standing of HD2-2 is resolved against the configuration before subjects are built. The hooks a record identifies are the hooks the identity half of the agreement of HD2-3 selects, the command verdict and the name form verdict each standing as that obligation states and every standing verdict required to agree and at least one verdict required to stand, the event check of the agreement standing aside for this resolution alone. Where the identified hooks all subscribe to one event, the record stands as a subject of that event exactly as a record naming that event stands, its agreement, its running, and its effects read under the standing rules with the resolved event standing in the place of the named one. Where the identified hooks subscribe to two events or more, or identify no hook, the record stands outside the examination as HD2-2 states, because the trace and the configuration together cannot place it.
- EV-3 The boundaries of the resolution. The resolution reads the configuration alone and never the record's call or its content, so a record is placed by what it identifies and not by what it ran against. A record whose identifiers disagree under HD2-3, a command naming one hook and a name form naming another, identifies no hook and stands outside. A resolved record counts in the matched evidence of every hook of its event exactly as a named record counts, and in the ran evidence and the effects of the hooks it identifies alone, the standing readings of HD2-4 and HD2-5 applying without change.
- EV-4 The stated standings that move. A recorded answer moves only where a recorded case carries a run record this resolution places and the case's hooks gain the evidence that record carries, and every such movement is a movement toward the reading the same record with an event field standing already receives, which the conformance suite's own evented cases prove. No other recorded answer moves, and the recorded battery of the command line stands unmoved.
- EV-5 The proof. The behaviours of this slice are proven by the named Rust pins test_an_eventless_run_record_counts_for_the_hook_its_command_names and test_an_eventless_blocking_record_credits_its_denial and test_an_eventless_record_identifying_hooks_of_two_events_stays_outside and test_an_eventless_record_identifying_no_hook_stays_outside and test_an_eventless_record_identifying_two_hooks_of_one_event_counts_for_both and test_a_disagreeing_eventless_record_stays_outside, standing in the tree in tests/eventless.rs over synthetic settings and session files the suite writes itself.

The slice stands closed as Round 4. The end of slice line share stands counted at the final head of the slice, with blank lines and comment only lines excluded and the recordings counted on neither side as data. The crate carries 3461 lines of Rust in its modules and 1265 lines in its Rust test suites, and no Python stands in the tree, so the implementation surface and the test surface both stand Rust whole. The non Rust remainder is none. The resolution stands proven by the six pins of EV-5, and the conformance suite passes on the closing tree with every recorded answer standing as it stood, no recorded case carrying a record this resolution places.

## Slice 4, the evidence format

The obligations below are the fourth slice, standing on the Rust alone as slice 3 stood. HD2-10 states that the class words are the join keys a later slice hands to the ledger as evidence and that a later slice joining verdicts to the ledger keys on the event and the matcher and the command and the class word, because the stable hook identifier is positional inside one configuration document and moves when the document moves. This slice is that later slice on this repo's side of the join. It publishes the evidence format the way the fleet's published evidence format stands published in its own repo, as a statement in this contract that is the whole contract a stranger needs to produce or consume the format. The probes below were run against the built binary before these obligations were written. The command line handed an evidence flag refuses it as a usage refusal today, so no evidence document stands anywhere. The examination of a synthetic configuration and session writes the two reports and nothing else, and the verdict of every hook stands inside the report alone, keyed by the positional identifier HD2-10 says moves, in a document whose remaining fields are written for a reader of the examination and not for a ledger joining verdicts across examinations.

- EVD-1 The scope and shape of this slice. The examination gains the evidence document, its emission, and its validation, and nothing else moves. The report format hookdoctor-report/1 stands unchanged in shape, every class word and evidence field standing as HD2-10 states, and no recorded answer the suites carry moves except the copies of the command line's usage line, which gain the evidence flag where EVD-4 states it. No new dependency stands in the crate.
- EVD-2 The format hookdoctor-evidence/1. The evidence document is a JSON object carrying the format name hookdoctor-evidence/1 and a verdicts list holding one verdict per configured hook in configuration order. Each verdict is an object carrying exactly the fields event and matcher and command and class and matched and ran and effects and first_ran_at and last_ran_at. The event and the command stand as the report entry spells them. The matcher stands as the matcher text, or as the empty text when the hook's group names no matcher, the empty and the missing matcher being the one standing HD2-3's name form already treats them as, so every verdict carries its join key whole as text. The class stands as the hook's class word under HD2-6. The matched and ran fields stand as the evidence counts of HD2-7 and the effects field stands as the effect counts object of the report entry, and the first_ran_at and last_ran_at fields stand as the report entry's dates stand, a text spelling or null. This statement is the whole contract a stranger needs to produce or consume the format.
- EVD-3 The join standing. The verdicts are the evidence HD2-10 hands to the ledger. A consumer joins a verdict to a configured hook on the event and the matcher and the command and reads the class word as the hook's verdict, exactly the keys HD2-10 states. The positional hook identifier of the report stands in no verdict, and no verdict carries a source label or a window, because a verdict states what the examination found about one hook and the labels of one run are not part of it.
- EVD-4 The emission. The command line gains one optional flag, --evidence, naming one local path and standing at most once as the other single path flags stand. When the flag stands, the evidence document is written to its path in the spelling HD2-11 states for JSON output, sorted keys and two space indentation and one trailing newline, beside the JSON report and the markdown report, and an evidence path that cannot be written refuses as a report path refuses. When the flag does not stand, no evidence document is written and every output of the run stands byte for byte as it stands today. The usage line of the command gains the evidence flag among its optional flags, and the copy of that line the command line suite carries moves with it as a mechanical consequence, no recorded case in the battery carrying the usage line.
- EVD-5 The validation. A validation of the evidence document rebuilds the expected document from the same configuration and traces and window and refuses a supplied document when any field differs or when a field is missing or when an unexpected field stands in the document, in the standing of the report validation of HD2-10.
- EVD-6 The proof. The behaviours of this slice are proven by the named Rust pins test_the_evidence_document_carries_one_verdict_per_hook_in_configuration_order and test_a_hook_whose_group_names_no_matcher_stands_with_an_empty_matcher_in_its_verdict and test_the_evidence_document_is_written_only_when_the_flag_stands and test_evidence_validation_refuses_a_tampered_class and test_evidence_validation_refuses_tampered_counts and test_evidence_validation_refuses_a_verdict_with_an_unexpected_field, standing in the tree in tests/evidence.rs over synthetic settings and session files the suite writes itself.

## Slice 5, the evidence check

The obligations below are the fifth slice, standing on the Rust alone as the slices before it stood. EVD-5 states the validation of the evidence document in the words of a supplied document, a validation that rebuilds the expected document from the same configuration and traces and window and refuses a supplied document when any field differs. That validation stands in the library and its pins hand it tampered documents directly, and the examination calls it on the document the same run just built, as a self check before the writing. No invocation of the binary accepts a supplied evidence document. The probes below were run against the built binary before these obligations were written. An evidence document written by one run, handed back to the binary with a flag naming the check, refuses as a usage refusal at exit code 2, the flag standing unknown to the shell, and the only path from a supplied document to the validation is a fresh examination whose own evidence the caller compares by hand. The holder of an evidence document, the ledger the verdicts are handed to under EVD-3 or an auditor holding the configuration and the traces the document claims, cannot ask the tool whether the document agrees. This slice gives the command line the check EVD-5 states.

- CEV-1 The scope and shape of this slice. The command line gains one optional flag and the validation mode it names, and nothing else moves. The report format hookdoctor-report/1 and the evidence format hookdoctor-evidence/1 stand unchanged in shape, every class word and evidence field standing as the standing obligations state, and no recorded answer the suites carry moves except the copies of the command line's usage line, which gain the check flag where CEV-4 states it. No new dependency stands in the crate.
- CEV-2 The flag and the mode. The flag stands as --check-evidence, naming one local path and standing at most once as the other single path flags stand, a repeated standing reading its last value. When the flag stands, the run is a validation and writes no examination output. The output and report flags are not required in this mode, and the output flag or the report flag or the evidence flag standing in this mode refuses as a usage refusal at exit code 2, because the mode takes no outputs and writes no evidence of its own. The configuration and session flags stand required as they stand, and the window and label flags stand as they stand, the labels standing in no part of the comparison because no verdict carries one under EVD-3.
- CEV-3 The validation. The mode ingests the configuration and the session files and the window exactly as the examination ingests them, reads the named document as JSON, a missing document refusing as a missing input refuses and a document that is not valid JSON refusing in the standing JSON wording, and validates the document under the standing validation of EVD-5, the expected document rebuilt from the same configuration and traces and window. A document that agrees exits 0 and prints nothing and writes nothing. A document that differs refuses with the standing words the evidence document disagrees with the examination, at the standing refusal exit.
- CEV-4 The usage line. The usage line of the command gains the check flag among its optional flags, standing as [--check-evidence PATH], and the copies of that line the command line suite carries move with it as a mechanical consequence, no recorded case in the battery carrying the usage line.
- CEV-5 The proof. The behaviours of this slice are proven by the named Rust pins test_a_supplied_evidence_document_validates_in_check_mode and test_check_mode_writes_no_examination_outputs and test_a_tampered_supplied_document_refuses_in_check_mode and test_a_missing_supplied_document_refuses_in_check_mode and test_check_mode_refuses_the_output_flags and test_check_mode_refuses_the_evidence_flag, standing in the tree in tests/evidence_check.rs over synthetic settings and session files the suite writes itself.

## Slice 6, the report check

The value reading skips whitespace and then dispatches on the standing character, an opening brace to the object reading and an opening bracket to the list reading and a quote to the string reading and the opening letters of the literal words to the literal reading and a minus or a digit to the number reading, and any other character refuses. - GR-1 The scope and shape of this slice. This slice states the grammar the decoder reads, the one grammar standing under both routes of the parser accounting, as obligations beneath PORT-2, whose sentence stands and whose probe findings this section writes out. No behaviour moves under this slice and no recorded answer moves and no new dependency stands in the crate. The route standings stand where they stand and are not restated, the duplicate refusal of R2-1 and the surrogate tolerance of RFIX-4 and the digit limit of DL and the pair standings of JV standing among them.
- GR-2 The document. A document stands as exactly one value of any kind, an object or a list or a text or a number or a truth value or null, with whitespace standing around it as GR-4 states, and any content standing past the value refuses the document. The empty text refuses and a text carrying only whitespace refuses.
- GR-3 The literal words. The word true stands as the true truth value and the word false as the false and the word null as null. The word NaN stands as the not a number float and the word Infinity as the positive infinity float, and a minus sign standing before Infinity stands as the negative infinity and a minus sign standing before NaN stands as the not a number float. No other word stands as a value, and a word standing partway refuses and a literal word carrying a letter past its end refuses.
- GR-4 The whitespace. The whitespace the decoder skips stands as exactly four characters, the space and the tab and the line feed and the carriage return, at every seat where whitespace is skipped. Any other character standing at such a seat stands as content, and the reading refuses where a value or a punctuation mark is required at that seat.
- GR-5 The number token shape. A number token stands as an optional minus and an integer part and an optional fraction and an optional exponent. The integer part stands as a single zero or as a digit run led by a digit other than zero. The fraction stands as a point with at least one digit after it. The exponent stands as the letter e in either case with an optional sign and at least one digit after it. A digit standing after a leading zero refuses, and a point with no digit after it refuses, and an exponent with no digit after it refuses, and a minus with no digit after it refuses, and a leading point refuses, and a leading plus refuses.
- GR-6 The number standings. An integer token, one carrying no fraction and no exponent, stands as the integer its digits name under the storage PORT-2 states, and its digit limit at the sampling route stands as DL states. A token carrying a fraction or an exponent stands as a float, its value standing as the nearest value the machine float holds, a magnitude past the float range standing as the infinity of its sign and a magnitude below the smallest subnormal standing as the zero of its sign. The token standing as a minus and a zero with no fraction and no exponent stands as the integer zero, and the same token carrying a fraction or an exponent stands as the negative zero float.
- GR-7 The string shape. A string stands between double quotes, and a single quote opens no string. Inside a string a character stands as itself, except that the quote ends the string and the backslash opens an escape and a raw control character below the character U+0020 refuses the document. A raw character of any other kind stands as itself, a character past the basic plane among them. The text a string names stands as the decoded text, and an object key reads under the same shape.
- GR-8 The escapes. The short escapes stand as their characters, the quote and the backslash and the solidus and the backspace and the form feed and the line feed and the carriage return and the tab. Any other character standing after a backslash refuses.
